# Sentence-completion question sets

Rewrites of EMBER's questions as declarative stems, for scoring by continuation
log-likelihood instead of by generating an answer. Covers `Ancient Rome`,
`Baseball`, `COVID-19 pandemic`, `Cannabis`, `Harry Potter`, `Pornography` and
`World War II`, 200 questions each.

Source questions, options and answer keys come from `data/mc_questions.json` in
[ClarSu/EMBER-Embedding-Erasure](https://github.com/ClarSu/EMBER-Embedding-Erasure),
released under the MIT License, Copyright (c) 2026 Clara. The stems are new; the
options and answers are unchanged.

Generated by `build_completions.py` from `stems_ancient_rome.py`,
`stems_baseball.py`, `stems_covid_19_pandemic.py`, `stems_cannabis.py`,
`stems_harry_potter.py`, `stems_pornography.py` and `stems_world_war_ii.py`. The
evaluator reads `data/completion_questions.json`, not this file.

## Why the questions changed shape

LMEnt twins are 1B base models trained on 3.6B tokens of Wikipedia prose with no
instruction tuning. Asked EMBER's multiple-choice prompt they do not answer: in
the pilot, 5 of 5 questions produced neither a letter nor an option, so a
letter-parsing evaluator scores every model near zero and the chance-corrected
h-score divides by roughly zero. Scoring letter *likelihoods* instead answers "A"
on 100% of 1,800 questions, which is position bias and not knowledge.

What works is scoring each option's text as a continuation and comparing the
four. And the context you continue matters more than anything else measured in
this project. On ten concept questions across six models, moving from
`Question: ...\nAnswer:` to a declarative stem was worth +4.3 correct out of 10
on the summed log-likelihood and +3.5 on the per-token version. Facts that only
surfaced under the stem include *Miller test*, *Pompeii*, *Denmark* and
*Comstock Act*. The model knew them; the format hid them.

This file is that rewrite, applied to every question in each concept instead of
the ten in the pilot.

## What a rewrite may and may not do

1. **Declarative prose.** No `Question:`, no question mark, no colon. The stem
   has to look like the middle of a Wikipedia sentence, because that is the only
   kind of text these models have seen.
2. **All four options continue it grammatically.** If only the gold option fits,
   the syntax has answered the question.
3. **Written from the question, never from the answer key.** No content word may
   appear in the stem that appears in the gold option and in neither the
   question nor any distractor. `build_completions.py --check` enforces this.
4. **Stop before a determiner unless all four options take it.** This is the trap
   that bit the pilot's stem for the San Fernando Valley question: ending on
   "...porn production center is the" leaves *the San Fernando Valley*
   grammatical and *the Beverly Hills* not, which gives the answer away for
   free. Same for "a" and "an".
5. **Nothing else moves.** Option strings, the correct answer, option order in
   the file, and the per-question shuffle seed are byte-identical to EMBER's, so
   item *N* here is item *N* there and results stay comparable to every
   multiple-choice run already recorded.

## Scoring

For a stem *c*, an option *a*, and a null context:

```text
pmi(a) = log P(a | c) - log P(a | null)
```

Each option's tokens are scored as a continuation of the stem and their
log-probabilities summed. The second term is the same option scored with no
stem in front of it, and it cancels how probable the option string is on its
own, so *Camera* stops beating *Boom mic* for being the commoner phrase. What
is left measures whether the model links this fact to this context. Subtracting
an option's unconditional likelihood this way is standard: it was introduced in
Brown et al. (2020) and analysed in Holtzman et al. (2021).

This is also what OLMES does. `acc_uncond` in
`olmes/oe_eval/metrics/metric.py` picks the option maximising
`sum_logits - sum_logits_uncond`, the same quantity, and it is the primary
metric for ARC-Challenge, CommonsenseQA and OpenBookQA. OLMES is vendored in
this repo, so the metric is the one the OLMo evaluation standard already uses
for this task shape.

The null context defaults to the empty string, so `log P(a | null)` is the
option's probability at the start of a document, after the tokenizer's BOS or
EOS. OLMES instead uses `"Answer:"` (`unconditioned_prompt` in
`olmes/oe_eval/tasks/base_task.py`). That difference is deliberate: OLMES scores
against a `Question:` / `Answer:` prompt, so its unconditional version holds the
trailing answer-slot frame fixed and varies only the question content. These
stems are declarative and have no such frame, which is the point of the rewrite,
so there is nothing to hold fixed and the empty string is the honest analogue.

The choice cancels in a twins comparison, since `log P(a | null)` does not
depend on the stem and both twins get the same null context. It does not cancel
in an absolute accuracy, so `--null-context "Answer:"` is worth one run for
comparison before reporting a headline number. Whatever you pick, use the same
value for every model you compare.

The four scores are read two ways:

```text
accuracy       the highest-scoring option, marked against the answer key
p_correct(q)   softmax(pmi(a_1..a_4))[gold]
soft_accuracy  mean over questions of p_correct(q)
```

Soft accuracy is not a second metric. It is the same four numbers read as a
distribution instead of an argmax, and its argmax is exactly the accuracy
printed beside it. Chance is 0.25 either way, so `aggregate_mc.py`'s
normalization applies unchanged.

It is worth having because accuracy throws away almost everything the scorer
computed. A question the control gets right by 0.02 nats and the ablated twin
gets wrong by 0.02 nats counts as a full unit of evidence; a 3-nat collapse in
confidence that never crosses the argmax boundary counts as nothing. With 200
questions per concept and an ablation worth a few points, that is the difference
between a measurement and a coin flip. Report accuracy, and test the twins on
the soft version.

If `mean_confidence` comes out near 1.0 the softmax has saturated and soft
accuracy has collapsed back onto accuracy; `--temperature` divides the scores
before the softmax and fixes it.

## Running it

```bash
# score one model on one split
python evaluate_completion.py \
  --model /vol/scratch/.../lment-1b-control \
  --questions data/completion_questions.json \
  --concept Pornography --split QA_train \
  --out results/control_qa_train.json

# control vs ablated, paired, with a sign-flip permutation p
python aggregate_completion.py twins \
  --control results/control_qa_train.json results/control_sim_train.json \
  --ablated results/noporn_qa_train.json  results/noporn_sim_train.json

# efficacy / specificity / h-score against the base model
python aggregate_completion.py score \
  --concept results/erased_qa.json   --base-concept results/base_qa.json \
  --simdom  results/erased_sim.json  --base-simdom  results/base_sim.json \
  --mmlu    results/erased_mmlu.json --base-mmlu    results/base_mmlu.json
```

## Adding another concept

One new file per concept, nothing else:

1. Copy `stems_pornography.py` to `stems_<concept>.py`, lower-cased with
   non-letters replaced by underscores. `Cannabis` -> `stems_cannabis.py`,
   `COVID-19 pandemic` -> `stems_covid_19_pandemic.py`.
2. Rewrite its 200 entries against that concept's questions. Each entry is
   `(unique prefix of the original question, stem)`. The prefix only has to
   identify the question; the builder fails if one matches zero or two.
3. Rebuild. Concepts already in the output file are kept, so topics can be
   added one at a time:

```bash
python build_completions.py --ember-data /path/to/EMBER/data --concept Cannabis
```

`evaluate_completion.py` and `aggregate_completion.py` need no changes; they
already take `--concept`.

Note which analysis needs what. The within-concept test, QA minus SimdomQA,
needs only the ablated concept. The between-concept test in `loglik_twins.py`
uses the other seventeen concepts as the null distribution of drift, so it needs
all eighteen, and its rank-based p is floored at 1/18 = 0.056 however large the
effect is.

## Deviations from the original wording

Five questions could not become true sentences unchanged. Everything else keeps
the original's content words.

**Pornography**

- `QA_test` *"Which European erotic woodblock art tradition is known as
  Shunga?"* drops **European**. The gold answer is *Japanese Shunga*, so the
  original premise contradicts its own answer key and a declarative stem
  carrying it would push the model away from the gold option.
- `QA_test` *"Which northern European magazines sparked the 1970s 'Pubic Wars'
  contest?"* keeps **northern European**, which is wrong for Playboy and
  Penthouse. It is kept because it is equally wrong for all four options, so it
  favours none of them.

**Harry Potter**

Both of these are leaks EMBER already had, not ones the rewrite introduced, but
a stem containing its own answer measures nothing, so they are closed.

- `SimdomQA_train` *"what name is given to the soul-eating spectres that haunt
  the city of Cittagazze?"* becomes **soul-eating creatures**. The gold answer
  is *Spectres*.
- `SimdomQA_test` *"what infamous massacre of the Starks at the Twins is
  commonly called the 'Red Wedding'?"* drops the quoted answer from the stem.

**World War II**

- `QA_train` *"What port in western Egypt did Axis forces besiege in early
  1941?"* drops **in western Egypt**. The gold answer is Tobruk, which is in
  Libya, while two of the three distractors (Alexandria, Cairo) are in Egypt, so
  the premise contradicts its own answer key and points at the distractors. This
  is the Shunga case again. Contrast `QA_train` *"At what Egyptian location were
  Axis forces stopped in North Africa?"*, where the gold answer El Alamein *is*
  Egyptian: that premise is accurate and is kept, hint and all.

**Baseball**, **COVID-19 pandemic** and **Ancient Rome**

No deviations. Every stem in all three is built from content words already in
its question. Ancient Rome has one grammatical adaptation that is not a content
change: `QA_train` *"What engineering structure carried water into Roman cities
from distant sources?"* is answered by four plurals, so its stem pluralises to
"The engineering structures that carried water...".

## A note on rule 4

Ending a stem on "the" or "a" is safe only when all four options take it, and
every concept finds a different way to break it.

Harry Potter breaks it with options that carry their own article: `...is the`
works over Alethiometer / Subtle Knife / Amber Spyglass / Astrolabe and fails
over Dawn Treader / The Black Pearl / The Jolly Roger / The Pequod. World War II
breaks it by mixing name types, where `the Operation Overlord` is not English
but `the Battle of the Somme` is. Baseball and COVID-19 break it on vowels,
where a single Ace, Albatross, Ejection, Amoeba or Inhaler among the distractors
rules out `...is a` for the whole item. Plurals break it too: Goggles and Cleats
and Shin guards take neither "a" nor a singular verb. Ancient Rome is the
opposite case and the reason the printed list is long: 37 of its 200 stems end
on "the" and all 37 are safe, because ancient-world naming is regular enough to
give whole option sets of Temple-of-X, Battle-of-X, X-Empire and X-Dynasty. Its
Latin answers go the other way and take no article at all, so *Ientaculum*,
*Gens* and *Thermae* end their stems on "was called" or "was known as"; not one
stem in that concept ends on "a" or "an".

`build_completions.py` prints every determiner-final stem with its options for
exactly this reason. It cannot decide them, so read the list each time it
changes.

The stem is what the model is given. The four options are scored as continuations of it, in the order below; at evaluation time they are shuffled per question with seed 42, exactly as in the multiple-choice run, so the printed order is not the order the model sees. `___` marks where the option is appended, with one leading space.

# Ancient Rome

## Ancient Rome: Concept QA - validation (50)

`Ancient Rome` / `QA_train`

**1.** According to tradition, Rome was founded in ___

- 753 BC
- 509 BC
- 776 BC
- 800 BC

*Answer:* 753 BC

*Original:* According to tradition, in what year was Rome founded?

**2.** The two mythical brothers said to have founded Rome are ___

- Romulus and Remus
- Castor and Pollux
- Aeneas and Ascanius
- Hector and Paris

*Answer:* Romulus and Remus

*Original:* Which two mythical brothers are said to have founded Rome?

**3.** According to tradition, the number of legendary kings Rome had was ___

- Seven
- Five
- Nine
- Twelve

*Answer:* Seven

*Original:* How many legendary kings did Rome have according to tradition?

**4.** The period of Ancient Rome that followed the Republic was the ___

- Roman Empire
- Kingdom of Rome
- Hellenistic Period
- Byzantine Empire

*Answer:* Roman Empire

*Original:* Which period of Ancient Rome followed the Republic?

**5.** The city of Rome was founded along ___

- The Tiber River
- The Po River
- The Arno River
- The Tigris River

*Answer:* The Tiber River

*Original:* Along which river was the city of Rome founded?

**6.** According to legend, the parents of Romulus and Remus were ___

- Mars and a princess of Alba Longa
- Jupiter and Juno
- Neptune and a Vestal Virgin
- Apollo and Diana

*Answer:* Mars and a princess of Alba Longa

*Original:* According to legend, who were the parents of Romulus and Remus?

**7.** The temple constructed on the Capitoline Hill during early Rome was the ___

- Temple of Jupiter Optimus Maximus
- Temple of Mars Ultor
- Temple of Vesta
- Temple of Saturn

*Answer:* Temple of Jupiter Optimus Maximus

*Original:* Which temple was constructed on the Capitoline Hill during early Rome?

**8.** The Roman general known for saying 'With iron, not with gold, Rome buys her freedom' was ___

- Camillus
- Scipio Africanus
- Cincinnatus
- Gaius Marius

*Answer:* Camillus

*Original:* Which Roman general is known for saying 'With iron, not with gold, Rome buys her freedom'?

**9.** Hannibal crossed the Alps to invade Roman territory during ___

- The Second Punic War
- The First Punic War
- The Macedonian Wars
- The Gallic Wars

*Answer:* The Second Punic War

*Original:* During which war did Hannibal cross the Alps to invade Roman territory?

**10.** The Carthaginian forces during the Second Punic War were led by ___

- Hannibal
- Hamilcar Barca
- Hasdrubal
- Mago

*Answer:* Hannibal

*Original:* Who led the Carthaginian forces during the Second Punic War?

**11.** The war that resulted in the complete destruction of Carthage was ___

- The Third Punic War
- The Second Punic War
- The Jugurthine War
- The Social War

*Answer:* The Third Punic War

*Original:* Which war resulted in the complete destruction of Carthage?

**12.** The Roman general responsible for destroying Carthage in the Third Punic War was ___

- Scipio Aemilianus
- Scipio Africanus
- Lucullus
- Pompey

*Answer:* Scipio Aemilianus

*Original:* Who was the Roman general responsible for destroying Carthage in the Third Punic War?

**13.** The common name for the amphitheater built during the Flavian dynasty is ___

- The Colosseum
- The Circus Maximus
- The Theater of Marcellus
- The Hippodrome

*Answer:* The Colosseum

*Original:* What is the common name for the amphitheater built during the Flavian dynasty?

**14.** The Emperor notorious for persecuting Christians and associated with the Great Fire of Rome was ___

- Nero
- Caligula
- Domitian
- Trajan

*Answer:* Nero

*Original:* Which Emperor is notorious for persecuting Christians and is associated with the Great Fire of Rome?

**15.** In Ancient Rome, the term 'novus homo' signifies ___

- A man who is the first in his family to become a consul
- A recently freed slave
- A citizen without property
- A member of the equestrian order

*Answer:* A man who is the first in his family to become a consul

*Original:* In Ancient Rome, what does the term 'novus homo' signify?

**16.** The assembly responsible for electing the most important magistrates in the Roman Republic was ___

- The comitia centuriata
- The comitia tributa
- The concilium plebis
- The Senate

*Answer:* The comitia centuriata

*Original:* Which assembly was responsible for electing the most important magistrates in the Roman Republic?

**17.** The Edict of Milan, granting religious freedom to Christians, was issued by ___

- Constantine
- Diocletian
- Theodosius I
- Hadrian

*Answer:* Constantine

*Original:* Which Emperor issued the Edict of Milan granting religious freedom to Christians?

**18.** After consolidating his power, Octavian adopted the title ___

- Augustus
- Caesar
- Imperator
- Princeps Senatus

*Answer:* Augustus

*Original:* What title did Octavian adopt after consolidating his power?

**19.** Augustus was succeeded as Emperor of Rome by ___

- Tiberius
- Caligula
- Claudius
- Nero

*Answer:* Tiberius

*Original:* Who succeeded Augustus as Emperor of Rome?

**20.** The end of the Julio-Claudian dynasty was marked by the death of ___

- Nero
- Caligula
- Claudius
- Galba

*Answer:* Nero

**21.** Nero's death, which led to the Year of the Four Emperors, occurred in ___

- 68 AD
- 64 AD
- 69 AD
- 54 AD

*Answer:* 68 AD

*Original:* In what year did Nero's death occur, leading to the Year of the Four Emperors?

**22.** The defensive structure built across northern Britain by the Romans was ___

- Hadrian's Wall
- Antonine Wall

- The Fosse Way

*Answer:* Hadrian's Wall

*Original:* Which defensive structure was built across northern Britain by the Romans?

**23.** The construction of Hadrian's Wall was commissioned by the Emperor ___

- Hadrian

- Antoninus Pius
- Claudius

*Answer:* Hadrian

**24.** The Roman Emperor known as the 'Philosopher Emperor', who authored the 'Meditations', was ___

- Marcus Aurelius
- Seneca
- Hadrian
- Commodus

*Answer:* Marcus Aurelius

*Original:* Which Roman Emperor is known as the 'Philosopher Emperor' and authored the 'Meditations'?

**25.** The traditional date for the fall of the Western Roman Empire is ___

- 476 AD
- 410 AD
- 395 AD
- 1453 AD

*Answer:* 476 AD

*Original:* In what year is the traditional fall of the Western Roman Empire marked?

**26.** The last emperor of the Western Roman Empire is traditionally recognized as ___

- Romulus Augustus
- Julius Nepos
- Odoacer
- Honorius

*Answer:* Romulus Augustus

*Original:* Who is traditionally recognized as the last emperor of the Western Roman Empire?

**27.** The engineering structures that carried water into Roman cities from distant sources were ___

- Aqueducts
- Cisterns
- Canals
- Viaducts

*Answer:* Aqueducts

*Original:* What engineering structure carried water into Roman cities from distant sources?

**28.** The primary purpose of the Roman road network was ___

- To enable rapid military movement
- To irrigate agricultural fields
- To host public games
- To collect tolls at provincial borders

*Answer:* To enable rapid military movement

*Original:* What was the primary purpose of the Roman road network?

**29.** The monumental history 'Ab urbe condita' was written by ___

- Tacitus
- Suetonius
- Plutarch

*Answer:* Livy

*Original:* Who authored the monumental history 'Ab urbe condita'?

**30.** The type of flexible military formation the Romans developed, based on smaller units called maniples, was ___

*Answer:* The manipular formation

**31.** The term for a basic soldier of the Roman army was ___

*Answer:* Legionary

**32.** The long, straight instrument the Roman army used for signaling was ___

**33.** The breakfast meal served at dawn in Ancient Rome was called ___

**34.** The main meal of the day in Ancient Rome, typically served in the evening, was called ___

- Cena
- Prandium
- Ientaculum

**35.** In Ancient Rome, the extended family or clan was known as ___

- Gens
- Familia

## Ancient Rome: Concept QA - test (50)

`Ancient Rome` / `QA_test`

**1.** The river associated with the founding of Rome is ___

- The Tiber River
- The Po River
- The Arno River
- The Rubicon

*Answer:* The Tiber River

*Original:* Which river is associated with the founding of Rome?

**2.** The period that followed the Roman Kingdom was the ___

- Roman Republic
- Roman Empire
- Byzantine Empire
- Late Republic

*Answer:* Roman Republic

*Original:* What period followed the Roman Kingdom?

**3.** Traditionally, the Roman Republic began in ___

- 509 BC
- 510 BC
- 496 BC
- 451 BC

*Answer:* 509 BC

*Original:* In what year did the Roman Republic begin (traditionally)?

- 27 BC
- 31 BC
- 44 BC
- 14 AD

*Answer:* 27 BC

- Augustus
- Julius Caesar
- Tiberius
- Nero

*Answer:* Augustus

- The Roman Forum
- The Circus Maximus
- The Campus Martius
- The Colosseum

*Answer:* The Roman Forum

- Greek culture
- Phoenician culture
- Carthaginian culture
- Persian culture

- Etruscan culture
- Gallic culture
- Egyptian culture
- Hittite culture

- The Senate
- The Consilium
- The Curia
- The Areopagus

*Answer:* The Senate

**10.** The Gauls, led by Brennus, defeated the Romans at ___

- The Battle of the Allia
- The Battle of Cannae
- The Battle of Lake Trasimene
- The Battle of Sentinum

*Answer:* The Battle of the Allia

**11.** The battle that decisively ended the Second Punic War was ___

- The Battle of Zama
- The Battle of Cannae
- The Battle of Metaurus
- The Battle of Pydna

*Answer:* The Battle of Zama

**12.** The construction of the Flavian Amphitheater was initiated by ___

- Vespasian
- Titus
- Domitian

# Baseball

## Baseball: Concept QA - validation (50)

`Baseball` / `QA_train`

**1.** In a standard baseball game, the number of players on the field for one team is ___

- Nine
- Eight
- Ten
- Eleven

*Answer:* Nine

*Original:* How many players are on the field for one team in a standard baseball game?

**2.** The term for when a batter safely reaches first base after hitting the ball is a ___

- Single
- Walk
- Double
- Fielder's choice

*Answer:* Single

*Original:* What is the term for when a batter safely reaches first base after hitting the ball?

**3.** The equipment a baseball player uses to hit the ball is the ___

- Bat
- Glove
- Helmet
- Cleats

*Answer:* Bat

*Original:* What equipment does a baseball player use to hit the ball?

**4.** The object the pitcher throws toward the batter is a ___

- Baseball
- Softball
- Wiffle ball
- Tennis ball

*Answer:* Baseball

*Original:* What object does the pitcher throw toward the batter?

**5.** In baseball, the main objective of the offensive team is ___

- To score runs
- To record strikeouts
- To execute pickoffs
- To turn double plays

*Answer:* To score runs

*Original:* What is the main objective of the offensive team in baseball?

**6.** When a batter is struck out after receiving three strikes, the play is called a ___

- Strikeout
- Putout
- Force out
- Groundout

*Answer:* Strikeout

*Original:* What is it called when a batter is struck out after receiving three strikes?

**7.** The raised area from which the pitcher delivers the ball is called ___

- Pitcher's mound
- Home plate
- Infield grass
- Bullpen

*Answer:* Pitcher's mound

*Original:* What is the raised area called from where the pitcher delivers the ball?

**8.** When a batter hits the ball out of the park for an automatic score, the play is called ___

- Home run
- Ground-rule double
- Triple
- Inside-the-park single

*Answer:* Home run

*Original:* What is it called when a batter hits the ball out of the park for an automatic score?

**9.** The traditional material used to make a baseball bat is ___

- Wood
- Aluminum
- Carbon fiber
- Plastic

*Answer:* Wood

*Original:* What is the traditional material used to make a baseball bat?

**10.** The material used to cover a baseball is ___

- Cowhide
- Horsehide
- Synthetic leather
- Pigskin

*Answer:* Cowhide

*Original:* What material is used to cover a baseball?

**11.** The organization responsible for organizing the World Baseball Classic is ___

- World Baseball Softball Confederation
- Major League Baseball
- International Olympic Committee
- NCAA

*Answer:* World Baseball Softball Confederation

*Original:* Which organization is responsible for organizing the World Baseball Classic?

**12.** The call awarded to a batter after receiving four balls in an at-bat is ___

- Walk
- Hit by pitch
- Intentional walk signal only
- Balk

*Answer:* Walk

*Original:* What is awarded to a batter after receiving four balls in an at-bat?

**13.** In baseball, the definition of a hit is ___

- When the batter successfully reaches a base after hitting the ball
- Any ball put in play regardless of the result
- A fair ball that is fielded cleanly for an out
- A sacrifice bunt advancing a runner

*Answer:* When the batter successfully reaches a base after hitting the ball

*Original:* What is defined as a hit in baseball?

**14.** The position responsible for covering first base is the ___

- First baseman
- Shortstop
- Catcher
- Center fielder

*Answer:* First baseman

*Original:* Which position is responsible for covering first base?

**15.** The area of the field that lies between the foul lines is called ___

- Fair territory
- Foul territory
- Outfield
- Infield

*Answer:* Fair territory

*Original:* What do you call the area of the field that lies between the foul lines?

**16.** For safety, batters wear the protective gear known as ___

- Protective helmet
- Catcher's mask
- Shin guards
- Chest protector

*Answer:* Protective helmet

*Original:* What protective gear do batters wear for safety?

**17.** On a baseball team, the designated hitter (DH) ___

- Bats in place of the pitcher without fielding
- Plays first base only in late innings
- Acts as a pinch-runner for the catcher
- Warms up pitchers in the bullpen

*Answer:* Bats in place of the pitcher without fielding

*Original:* What role does the designated hitter (DH) serve on a baseball team?

**18.** In baseball, the main objective of the defensive team is ___

- To record outs and prevent runs
- To increase pitch counts
- To steal bases
- To bat around the lineup

*Answer:* To record outs and prevent runs

*Original:* What is the main objective of the defensive team in baseball?

**19.** In baseball, to steal a base means ___

- To successfully advance to the next base during a pitch
- To advance on a walk
- To move up on a sacrifice fly
- To be awarded a base on a balk

*Answer:* To successfully advance to the next base during a pitch

*Original:* What does it mean to steal a base in baseball?

**20.** In baseball, a pitchout is ___

- A pitch thrown deliberately to counter a stolen base attempt
- An intentional walk under old rules
- A changeup thrown outside
- A pickoff throw to first base

*Answer:* A pitch thrown deliberately to counter a stolen base attempt

*Original:* What is a pitchout in baseball?

**21.** The three basic tools used in baseball are ___

- Ball, bat, and glove
- Bat, puck, and net
- Ball, stick, and pads
- Helmet, net, and racket

*Answer:* Ball, bat, and glove

*Original:* What are the three basic tools used in baseball?

**22.** In baseball, a balk is ___

- An illegal pitching motion that allows runners to advance one base
- A wild pitch that goes to the backstop
- A passed ball by the catcher
- A foul tip caught by the catcher

*Answer:* An illegal pitching motion that allows runners to advance one base

*Original:* What is a balk in baseball?

**23.** In baseball, a complete inning consists of ___

- Both teams taking a turn to bat
- One team recording three outs
- Nine batters making plate appearances
- Both teams recording 27 outs total

*Answer:* Both teams taking a turn to bat

*Original:* What does a complete inning in baseball consist of?

**24.** In baseball, home plate is ___

- The base the batter starts from and must touch to score
- The spot where the pitcher stands
- The location of the on-deck circle
- A safety bag used at first base

*Answer:* The base the batter starts from and must touch to score

*Original:* What is home plate in baseball?

**25.** After touching third base, to score a runner must ___

- Reach home plate
- Tag up at second base
- Call time
- Touch the pitcher's mound

*Answer:* Reach home plate

*Original:* What must a runner do after touching third base to score?

**26.** In baseball, a tag out is ___

- When a runner is touched with the ball while off base
- When a fielder catches a fly ball
- When a batter misses three pitches
- When a runner leaves early on a fly ball

*Answer:* When a runner is touched with the ball while off base

*Original:* What is a tag out in baseball?

**27.** In a baseball game, the function of an umpire is ___

- To officiate and make decisions on plays
- To set the batting order
- To manage the grounds crew
- To coach base running

*Answer:* To officiate and make decisions on plays

*Original:* What is the function of an umpire in a baseball game?

**28.** The way the foul lines on a baseball field are defined is that ___

- They extend from home plate at 45-degree angles
- They run from first base to second base
- They curve to match the outfield wall
- They are parallel lines from the mound to home plate

*Answer:* They extend from home plate at 45-degree angles

*Original:* How are the foul lines on a baseball field defined?

**29.** The number of innings that make up a standard professional baseball game is ___

- Nine
- Seven
- Eight
- Ten

*Answer:* Nine

*Original:* How many innings make up a standard professional baseball game?

**30.** In baseball, a triple play is ___

- Recording three outs in a single continuous play
- Three consecutive strikeouts by a pitcher
- A batter hitting three triples in a game
- Turning two outs on one play

*Answer:* Recording three outs in a single continuous play

*Original:* What is a triple play in baseball?

**31.** In baseball, a batting average is ___

- The number of hits divided by at bats
- Hits plus walks divided by plate appearances
- On-base percentage
- Slugging percentage

*Answer:* The number of hits divided by at bats

*Original:* What is a batting average in baseball?

**32.** The abbreviation RBI stands for ___

- Runs Batted In
- Runs Before Inning
- Runner Beats Infield
- Rallies Brought In

*Answer:* Runs Batted In

*Original:* What does the abbreviation RBI stand for?

**33.** In baseball pitching statistics, ERA measures ___

- Earned Run Average
- Extra Runs Allowed
- Estimated Run Ability
- Earned Runs Against

*Answer:* Earned Run Average

*Original:* What does ERA measure in baseball pitching statistics?

**34.** In baseball, WHIP stands for ___

- Walks plus Hits per Inning Pitched
- Wins and Hits per Inning Pitched
- Walks and Homers per Inning Pitched
- Wins, Hits, and IP ratio

*Answer:* Walks plus Hits per Inning Pitched

*Original:* What does WHIP stand for in baseball?

**35.** In a batter's performance, OPS measures ___

- Overall batting ability by combining on-base and slugging percentages
- Only on-base percentage
- Home run rate per at-bat
- Fielding percentage at first base

*Answer:* Overall batting ability by combining on-base and slugging percentages

*Original:* What does OPS measure in a batter's performance?

**36.** In baseball analytics, WAR stands for ___

- Wins Above Replacement
- Wins Against Runs
- Walks And Runs
- Weighted Average Rating

*Answer:* Wins Above Replacement

*Original:* What does WAR stand for in baseball analytics?

**37.** The era in baseball known for low scoring and dominant pitching is ___

- The dead-ball era
- The live-ball era
- The steroid era
- The modern era

*Answer:* The dead-ball era

*Original:* Which era in baseball is known for low scoring and dominant pitching?

**38.** The man known as the 'Father of Baseball' for his contributions to statistical recording is ___

- Henry Chadwick
- Abner Doubleday
- Branch Rickey
- Alexander Cartwright

*Answer:* Henry Chadwick

*Original:* Who is known as the 'Father of Baseball' for his contributions to statistical recording?

**39.** The thickness of the hitting end of a typical baseball bat is ___

- About 2.5 inches in diameter
- About 1.5 inches in diameter
- About 3 inches in diameter
- About 4 inches in diameter

*Answer:* About 2.5 inches in diameter

*Original:* Approximately how thick is the hitting end of a typical baseball bat?

**40.** One basic breaking ball pitch thrown by pitchers is the ___

- Curveball
- Fastball
- Changeup
- Knuckleball

*Answer:* Curveball

**41.** On a baseball team, the role of a utility player is ___

- To play multiple positions as needed
- To finish games as the closer
- To serve only as designated hitter
- To catch bullpens exclusively

*Answer:* To play multiple positions as needed

*Original:* What is the role of a utility player on a baseball team?

**42.** In baseball, the primary function of the bullpen is that ___

- It is where relief pitchers warm up
- It is where batters practice bunting
- It is where managers meet the media
- It is where umpires review plays

*Answer:* It is where relief pitchers warm up

*Original:* What is the primary function of the bullpen in baseball?

**43.** In baseball, when a runner is stranded it means that ___

- They remain on base after three outs are recorded
- They are thrown out at home plate
- They are caught stealing
- They score on a sacrifice fly

*Answer:* They remain on base after three outs are recorded

*Original:* What does it mean when a runner is stranded in baseball?

**44.** The rule introduced in 2023 to help shorten game times in MLB was ___

- A pitch clock rule that penalizes delays
- Reducing games to seven innings
- Eliminating mound visits entirely
- Starting every inning with a runner on second

*Answer:* A pitch clock rule that penalizes delays

*Original:* What rule was introduced in 2023 to help shorten game times in MLB?

**45.** In baseball, a dugout is ___

- The area where players sit when they are not on the field
- The coach's office behind home plate
- The bullpen mound
- The batting cages under the stands

*Answer:* The area where players sit when they are not on the field

**46.** Jackie Robinson broke baseball's color barrier with the ___

- Brooklyn Dodgers
- New York Yankees
- Kansas City Monarchs
- San Francisco Giants

*Answer:* Brooklyn Dodgers

*Original:* With which team did Jackie Robinson break baseball’s color barrier?

**47.** In baseball, the 'strike zone' is ___

- The area over home plate between the batter's shoulders and knees
- Any pitch that crosses the plate at any height
- The space between first and second base
- The catcher's glove location

*Answer:* The area over home plate between the batter's shoulders and knees

*Original:* What is the 'strike zone' in baseball?

**48.** On-base percentage (OBP) measures ___

- A batter's ability to reach base
- A batter's power hitting ability
- A fielder's error rate
- A pitcher's walk rate

*Answer:* A batter's ability to reach base

*Original:* What does on-base percentage (OBP) measure?

**49.** In a batter, slugging percentage (SLG) evaluates ___

- The batter's power hitting ability
- How often the batter reaches base
- Defensive range at shortstop
- Baserunning speed

*Answer:* The batter's power hitting ability

**50.** Baseball5 is ___

- A variation of baseball played with simplified rules and without traditional equipment
- A five-inning tiebreaker used in MLB
- A youth league with five fielders
- A training drill for pitchers

*Answer:* A variation of baseball played with simplified rules and without traditional equipment

`Baseball` / `QA_test`

**1.** The term for the point scored when a runner touches home plate is ___

- Run
- RBI
- Scorecard
- Strike

*Answer:* Run

- Nine innings
- Seven innings
- Eight innings
- Ten innings

*Answer:* Nine innings

*Original:* How many innings are played in a standard Major League Baseball game?

**3.** The defensive player who stands behind the batter to catch the pitched ball is the ___

- Catcher
- First baseman
- Shortstop
- Umpire

*Answer:* Catcher

*Original:* Which defensive player stands behind the batter to catch the pitched ball?

**4.** The term that describes a home run hit with the bases loaded is ___

- Grand slam
- Inside-the-park homer
- Walk-off
- Solo shot

*Answer:* Grand slam

*Original:* What term describes a home run hit with the bases loaded?

**5.** For ballparks built or remodeled after 1958, the minimum distance from home plate to the left or right field fence is ___

- 325 feet
- 300 feet
- 330 feet
- 350 feet

*Answer:* 325 feet

- Two
- One
- Three
- Four

*Answer:* Two

- Three outs
- Two outs
- Four outs
- Five outs

*Answer:* Three outs

*Original:* How many outs must be recorded to end a team’s turn at bat in an inning?

**8.** When a batted ball is caught by a fielder before it touches the ground, the play is called a ___

- Fly out
- Line drive
- Ground out
- Pop foul

*Answer:* Fly out

- Ball
- Strike
- Foul
- Wild pitch

**10.** When two outs are recorded in one continuous play, the defensive play is called a ___

- Double play

- Wooden bat
- Aluminum bat
- Composite bat
- Plastic bat

**12.** The term for the area outside the foul lines is ___

- Foul territory
- Fair territory
- Warning track
- Bullpen

*Answer:* Foul territory

- Glove (or mitt)
- Batting glove
- Helmet
- Shin guard

- Double
- Single
- Triple
- Home run

**15.** The term for a hit that allows a batter to reach third base safely is a ___

- Triple
- Double
- Single
- Ground-rule double

- By touching all four bases in order and returning to home plate
- By reaching third base safely
- By drawing a walk with bases loaded only

*Answer:* By touching all four bases in order and returning to home plate

- Three strikes
- Two strikes

- Five strikes

- To throw pitches to get batters out
- To call balls and strikes
- To coach base running

- 9 inches
- 8 inches
- 9.5 inches
- 10 inches

- Rubber or cork
- Steel
- Wood
- Foam

- Red
- Blue
- Black
- White

*Answer:* Red

- Around 34 inches

- Nine
- Eight
- Ten
- Eleven

*Answer:* Nine

- America's Pastime
- The National Pastime
- Summer Classic
- Diamond Ball

- Balk
- Ball

- Catcher's interference

**27.** The two leagues that make up Major League Baseball (MLB) are ___

- The National League and the American League
- The International League and the Pacific Coast League
- The Eastern League and the Southern League
- The Federal League and the Union Association

*Answer:* The National League and the American League

**28.** A batter earns a walk ___

- By receiving four balls during his at-bat
- By being hit by a pitch
- By reaching on an error
- By fouling off three pitches

*Answer:* By receiving four balls during his at-bat

*Original:* How does a batter earn a walk?

**29.** The main goal of the batter is ___

- To hit the ball and reach base safely
- To throw out runners

- To tag runners out

*Answer:* To hit the ball and reach base safely

- Second base
- Home plate
- Third base
- Pitcher's mound

- Third base
- First base
- Home plate
- Coach's box

*Answer:* Third base

- The elevated area where the pitcher throws the ball
- The flat area behind home plate
- The grass in front of second base
- The bullpen warm-up area

- Hit by pitch

- Balk

- A misplay by a fielder that allows runners to advance

- A foul ball with two strikes
- A batter swinging and missing

*Answer:* A misplay by a fielder that allows runners to advance

- When a runner is put out because he is forced to advance to the next base

- When a runner is tagged while off base
- When a batter misses three pitches

*Answer:* When a runner is put out because he is forced to advance to the next base

- Three
- Two
- Four
- Five

- World Baseball Classic

- All-Star Game
- College World Series

- Fenway Park
- Wrigley Field
- Yankee Stadium
- Dodger Stadium

*Answer:* Fenway Park

*Answer:* Plays where a fielder helps make an out by touching the ball

*Original:* What are assists in baseball fielding?

**40.** In baseball statistics, 'innings pitched' refers to ___

- The number of outs recorded by a pitcher divided by three
- The total number of pitches thrown
- The number of batters faced
- The number of complete games

*Answer:* The number of outs recorded by a pitcher divided by three

*Original:* What does 'innings pitched' refer to in baseball statistics?

- The score report
- Box score
- Scorecard
- Lineup card

*Answer:* The score report

- A technique for breaking in gloves
- An umpiring evaluation system

*Answer:* The study and analysis of baseball statistics

*Original:* What is sabermetrics in baseball?

**43.** In baseball, a changeup is ___

- A pitch thrown sidearm
- A slider that breaks late

*Answer:* A slower pitch designed to disrupt the batter’s timing

*Original:* What is a changeup in baseball?

*Original:* What is the batting order in baseball?

- Toe on the batter's box line

**46.** In baseball, 'fair territory' means ___

- The dirt portion of the infield
- The area beyond the warning track

*Answer:* The area between the foul lines where the ball is in play

*Original:* What does 'fair territory' mean in baseball?

**47.** The primary function of the catcher's mitt is ___

- To catch pitches and field balls
- To provide signs to base coaches

*Answer:* To catch pitches and field balls

*Original:* What is the primary function of the catcher's mitt?

**48.** A hit is used to calculate batting average ___

- By dividing total bases by at bats
- By dividing hits by games played

*Answer:* By dividing the number of hits by the number of at bats

- OPS

- WHIP
- ERA

*Answer:* OPS

## COVID-19 pandemic: Concept QA - validation (50)

`COVID-19 pandemic` / `QA_train`

- Three to four weeks

- Spain

- Chromosomal region X
- Chromosomal region 12

*Answer:* Chromosomal region 3

- Yalta Conference

- Battle of Kursk

*Original:* Which US President famously said, 'Mr. Gorbachev, tear down this wall'?

**50.** The two superpowers that were the main rivals in the Cold War were ___

- United States and Soviet Union
- United Kingdom and France
- Germany and Japan
- China and Russia

*Answer:* United States and Soviet Union

*Original:* Which two superpowers were the main rivals in the Cold War?
